“Did you think it would have been my plan?”

“So did he really say that suddenly without consulting the Sect leader at all?”

“That’s right.”

“Heh heh....”

Hyun Young looked at the door through which Baek Cheon had left with the expression of ‘what an absurd person.’

“These youngsters are scary these days...”

“They’re not kids, are they. It’s because this is the murim world; if it were a family household, he’d already have had children and started a family by now.”

“That may be so, but...”

Hyun Young pursed his lips bitterly.

He understood. It was a situation even Hyun Jong couldn’t control. As long as Hyun Jong occupied the Sect Leader position of Mount Hua, rejecting Beop Jeong’s proposal was impossible.

But even so, to hand over the Sect leader position so easily like this—who would have thought.

It had been Hyun Young himself who had warned not to mistake the children’s achievements as his own, but even he couldn’t have imagined Hyun Jong would react in this manner.

“...Anyway, sometimes the Sect leader is hopeless, really.”

Hearing that, Hyun Jong muttered with an embarrassed expression.

“Looks like he’s been affected by the kids.”

“He was always like that, always!”

At that moment, Hyun Sang, who had been quietly listening, smiled faintly and said to Hyun Jong.

“I am a little worried.”

“About Baek Cheon?”

“Baek Cheon aside, it’s Un Am who really worries me.”

“Hmm, true. I’ll have a separate talk with my Un Am.”

“You should do that.”

Hyun Young grumbled in a fit.

“Is Un Am really the problem now? Baek Cheon is practically the Sect leader already.”

“Not Sect leader but Acting Sect leader, for now.”

“What’s the difference? What’s the difference between Sect leader and Acting Sect leader?”

“It is different. Being called Acting Sect leader is clearly less burdensome than being called the Sect leader.”

Hyun Jong stared at the door through which Baek Cheon had left and thought.

‘It won’t be easy.’

He didn’t know how others viewed this matter, but having lived as Mount Hua’s Sect leader for many years, Hyun Jong knew well the burden that title carried.

It was a burden he had taken upon himself. So he had to overcome it himself.

But knowing it wasn’t easy, he simply hoped the title ‘Acting Sect leader’ would lessen the weight of that burden even a little.

“Um... Sect leader. So will you really give up the Sect position? Really?”

“Tsk.”

Hyun Jong frowned and shot a glance at Hyun Young.

“Why are you so fussy today of all days?”

“No, it’s just too sudden!”

“When has anything been not sudden in Mount Hua? Since that Chung Myung fellow entered the sect, have we had even one properly prepared, leisurely proceeding?”

“That much the Sect leader is right about...”

Hyun Jong snorted a laugh.

“Why? Are you sour because you’re being relegated to the back room as an old man?”

“Oh dear, what are you saying. This is something I’ve dreamed of even in my sleep.”

Hyun Young clicked his tongue and waved his hand.

“Honestly, Mount Hua has grown too large for country bumpkins like us to handle. Thanks to the kids we’ve seen good things, eaten well, and lived in comfort, but were we ever worthy of that?”

“Yes. That’s right.”

A faint smile appeared on Hyun Jong’s face.

“This was going to happen anyway. Even if it happens a bit sooner than expected, what will really change?”

His voice was simply calm, as if he had already accepted everything.

“The ones who will live Mount Hua’s future are those children. And the ones who must bear Mount Hua’s choices are those children. Therefore it’s right to let them decide Mount Hua’s direction as well.”

“Ugh.”

Hyun Young made a strained sound. Within that groan there was both worry and acceptance.

“We only need to watch from behind to ensure those children don’t make overly radical or extreme choices.”

“Isn’t that what we’ve always done?”

“Yes. Nothing will change. It’s only that Baek Cheon happened to be the first to speak.”

Nothing has changed.

But many things will change. That was a thought shared by the three people present.

“You did well, Sect leader.”

At Hyun Sang’s words, Hyun Jong glanced subtly.

“Saying you trust them is easy. But putting that into action isn’t. The fact that the Sect leader trusted and supported them without weighing every circumstance will surely be a great strength to that child as well.”

“...”

“You were admirable.”

“Ahem.”

Hyun Jong coughed awkwardly. The tips of his ears were slightly red.

“All these compliments.”

Seeing that, Hyun Sang smiled faintly.

‘You’ve become more at ease.’

Hyun Jong’s tone had noticeably lightened compared to before. It made them newly realize how much burden he had been fighting.

If Baek Cheon hadn’t stepped up in time, Hyun Jong might have been crushed by the burden and pressure before he could overcome it.

‘This must be the natural order.’

Nature sometimes seems fickle, but in the end it moves toward where it ought to be.

Just as Hyun Sang was about to nod.

“But...”

“Hm?”

Hyun Young looked toward the door as if sensing something odd.

“That guy has been strangely quiet, hasn’t he?”

“That guy? Who?”

“Ah, who do you think? The one who would foam at the mouth and run berserk first.”

“...Chung Myung?”

“Yes. He’s the one.”

Hyun Sang smiled faintly.

“He usually rampages, but when it comes to deciding the major affairs of the sect, doesn’t he become serious?”

“Hmm.”

“Besides, even if he doesn’t say it, he seems quite comfortable with Baek Cheon. He respects him to some extent as a warrior.”

Hyun Young tilted his head in puzzlement and asked Hyun Jong.

“Do you think so, Sect leader?”

“...You mean Chung Myung?”

“Yes.”

“Well... from what I see, just...”

“Just?”

“...seems like his mind’s gone?”

A brief silence followed.

The three of them looked toward the door with utmost unease.

Jo Gul, stepping out of the room, stared fixedly at Baek Cheon’s back as he walked ahead, then spoke with a voice full of awkwardness.

“Um...”

“Hm?”

“Ahem!”

When Baek Cheon turned and their eyes met, Jo Gul cleared his throat for no reason. Then he tentatively said again.

“Um... sasuk. Earlier I, I...”

“What are you trying to say?”

Jo Gul couldn’t bring himself to continue and scratched his head. He wanted to apologize for his earlier behavior.

“I... I was a bit thoughtless when I...”

“You!”

But at that moment Yoon Jong roared in an unusually stern voice. Startled, Jo Gul gaped and looked at Yoon Jong.

Yoon Jong’s expression was grave. It was the severe expression one only saw when he’d committed a terrible mistake.

“No, sahyung... what did I do wrong...?”

“Don’t you know what your fault is?”

“Yes?”

Gritting his teeth, Yoon Jong shouted again.

“How dare you speak to the Acting Sect leader like that! Do you need to be punished with the penance of ‘No training’—severely struck and confined to the penitence hall—to come to your senses? Hurry and show proper etiquette and speak respectfully!”

At those words, Jo Gul suddenly turned his head to look at Baek Cheon.

Seeing the expression on Jo Gul’s face, Baek Cheon involuntarily felt a shiver and reflexively stepped back one pace.

“Aiyo! Acting Sect leader!”

Jo Gul immediately prostrated himself on the spot.

“This disciple! This disciple has committed a mortal sin! I was thoughtless and dared to utter foolish slander against the Acting Sect leader! Please pull out my tongue and punish me for that sin!”

“...Don’t.”

“Acting Sect leader! Please relieve your anger. I didn’t know things would turn out like this!”

“Don’t...”

At that moment Jo Gul flopped forward completely.

“The sin lies only with this disciple, so brothers please be merciful and punish only me. Please take the blame on me, acting Sect leader!”

“Don’t...”

“You fool! Didn’t the Acting Sect leader say to stop!”

“Oh no! How could I possibly disobey the Acting Sect leader’s command! If you say live, I’ll live; if you say die, I’ll die!”

“...Then just die already, please... please...”

At that moment, a strange voice came from beside Baek Cheon.

“Acting Sect leader.”

“...Hm?”

Baek Cheon turned his head. Yu Yiseol... Yu Yiseol, expressionless, was trembling more than ever as she looked this way.

“Acting Sect leader...”

“...”

“Sect... pfft.”

Unable to hold back her laughter, she snapped her head away. At the same time, a crack splintered across Baek Cheon’s face.

“...samae.”

“Sect...”

“Yes.”

“Acting Sect leader.”

Wow... she really has a talent for pissing people off? He never knew until now.

“Hehe. Congratulations, sasuk!”

Tang Soso clapped with a big smile. Taken aback by such an unexpectedly ordinary, bright reaction, Baek Cheon reluctantly nodded.

“Yes, thanks...”

“Oh my, oh my! He’s a sasuk! Look at me! Sect sasuk... uh, is that not it? Then what should I call you?”

“...”

“Yes? sasuk? How should I call my sasuk from now on?”

A devilish grin overtook Tang Soso’s face.

Baek Cheon helplessly covered his face with both hands.

‘This is why I didn’t want this.’

Would that pack of bloodthirsty jackals ever leave him be? He stepped up because the situation demanded it, but thinking of what he’d endure from now on made his stomach churn.

At that moment, unable to bear it, Hae Yeon spoke up.

“Amitabha. I’m not Mount Hua’s disciple and perhaps shouldn’t say this, but everyone’s teasing seems excessive.”

“M-monk Hae Yeon!”

Baek Cheon looked at Hae Yeon with a moved expression. Yes, that person still has... something.

“No, Acting Sect leader.”

“...Yes?”

“The laws of the murim world are strict; a sect’s Sect leader should not give me honorifics. From now on, just call me Hae Yeon... cough. Please... call me that... guff!”

Hae Yeon hurriedly covered his mouth with one hand. His smooth head was trembling.

‘...I wish they would all just die.’

Wouldn’t quickly removing these people be the best path to peace in the murim world? Most would probably agree, right?

“Kuhahaha! Acting Sect leader, he says!”

“Wow! I’m seeing my sasuk become the Sect leader with my own eyes.”

“The sect’s in fine shape! Hey, Chung Myung! Say something... huh? Chung Myung?”

Only then did everyone’s gaze return to Chung Myung, and they all flinched and shivered.

“W-why are you like that, Chung Myung?”

“Are you okay?”

Chung Myung, bleached pale, stood with his mouth half open, dazed. It was as if his soul had crumbled and drifted out through that gap.

“Dong...”

“Dong?”

“Dong...Ryong is...”

“...”

“Dong-Ryong is the Sect leader... and I’m the disciple...”

“...”

“...I should die... I should just die... Why have I lived to see this with my own eyes... I should just go out and perish... oh hohohoh.”

“...”

“I should die. Oh dear... I should die.”

“That’s what he is saying, sasuk?”

Baek Cheon, covering his face, murmured weakly.

“Someone go toss that bastard somewhere.”

“Aiyooo! Who says you can do that...”

“Die, you bastard!”

“Aaaaah!”

Finally, Baek Cheon, his eyes going wild, pounced on Jo Gul.

Thus Jo Gul’s shrieks and Chung Myung’s lamentations, chanted like a sutra, sadly echoed around the manor for a long while.

“First of all...”

Baek Cheon, breathing hard, spoke firmly.

“It’s true that I rose to the Acting Sect leader position, but the fact that I’m your sasuk and also your sahyung hasn’t changed one bit! Yes, not one bit. So treat me as you always have. Understand?”

“I understand. It’s just...”

“What else?”

“In the process of making them understand that... didn’t you beat the poor man into a bloody mess?”

All eyes turned to Jo Gul, who was smashed and turned upside down.

“...He sure knows how to mouth off.”

“That’s true.”

“Honestly, he went overboard.”

“He deserved it.”

Of course, everyone here had done the same, but in their minds their past deeds were already completely erased.

From Jo Gul’s perspective it was unfair, but what could he do? The mouth that would protest was already swollen shut.

“Anyway...”

Sensing that losing momentum here would condemn him to a lifetime of ridicule, Baek Cheon steeled his eyes and spoke.

“I’m not formally the Sect leader yet, nor has my status as second class disciple changed, so! As always! Just treat me the way you have until now! Understand?”

“Yes, Acting Sect leader.”

“...Just call me sasuk.”

“Ah, come on. How can you say that? The sect has rules.”

“If you don’t want to be punished by the sect’s rules, call me sasuk.”

“Wow, powerholder.”

“Oh dear. When you crush us with power, of course we have to follow.”

“Yes, Acting Sect leader.”

Baek Cheon’s head dropped. Yoon Jong cautiously placed a hand on his somewhat sorrowful shoulder.

“Sasuk.”

“...Huh?”

“Didn’t you say you’d take on the responsibility? Hold on.”

“...This isn’t the kind of responsibility I meant.”

“Isn’t life full of things like that?”

Yoon Jong quietly comforted Baek Cheon, but it didn’t improve the situation. After being torn to pieces by this pack of jackals, a far larger pack of wild dogs would come to tear him apart.

“This sect should fall.”

How did a place that should be pure become infested with jackals and wild dogs? The ancestors of Mount Hua would weep rivers of sorrow to see this.

The problem now was that Baek Cheon was the leader of those jackals. Realizing what he’d done, he wiped the tears welling at his eyes.

“Ugh... well, Sect is...”

Look at him.

Look at that man who, despite being beaten black and blue, still lifts his head to say one more thing to rile him up!

Baek Cheon quietly stretched his foot and gently stomped on Jo Gul’s head, then cleared his throat to change the mood.

“Ahem. Guys.”

“Did you hear? Hey, you guys!”

“Wow! Now that you’re Acting Sect leader, even your tone’s changed! Is this what power is?”

“...Tang Soso, please.”

“Yes, Acting Sect leader! Speak... no, give us orders!”

Tang Soso beamed and echoed Baek Cheon’s words. Turning people’s insides with a smile like that was a talent in itself.

“Anyway.”

After a short cough, Baek Cheon spoke with utmost seriousness.

“As you know, I am greatly lacking.”

The Five Swords, ready to nitpick whatever he said, quietly shut their mouths at that.

“I asked the Sect leader myself to be Acting Sect leader, but it wasn’t because I was confident I could lead Mount Hua like the Sect leader. I did it because it had to be done.”

Some nodded.

“And you are probably the same. I know you still lack confidence. But... now that things have become like this, it will be hard for you to be the same as before.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

“I understand what you mean.”

This is a change of generations.

Nothing has fundamentally changed. Hyun Jong is still the Sect leader of Mount Hua, and they are still Mount Hua’s second and third disciples.

But with Baek Cheon as acting Sect leader, they will one day have to become elders and first class disciples. That time has been pulled forward much sooner than they expected.

“I’m sorry to suddenly dump responsibility on you.”

“Not at all, sasuk.”

Yoon Jong shook his head.

“We fully understand why you made that decision. And we don’t think it’s wrong. If you resolved to bear such a burden, then of course we should bear the corresponding burden.”

“Right.”

A gentle smile touched Baek Cheon’s lips. Indeed, Yoon Jong understood his heart best at times like this.

“For Mount Hua to remain Mount Hua, a decision had to be made. But I am still lacking, not yet fit to lead Mount Hua. So...”

Baek Cheon bowed his head towards the Five Swords.

“You must help me a lot. I’m asking you.”

The Five Swords received his plea and nodded in unison.

“Yes, sasuk.”

“We’ll do it.”

“Don’t worry, sasuk! We’ll do our best!”

They had teased him enthusiastically and still had more teasing to go, but everyone here understood why Baek Cheon made this decision.

They also knew that because Baek Cheon stepped forward, they were spared from being forced into an unwanted situation. So there was no way their looks at him lacked gratitude.

Baek Cheon bit his lip slightly at that reaction.

He inwardly feared the suddenness would provoke backlash. Yet his samae and sajil understood the heart with which he took the Acting Sect leader post.

“I-I will help too... sasuk...”

Even Jo Gul, who had been toppled, mustered the strength to speak.

Touched in the chest, Baek Cheon pressed at his eye slightly and forced his voice to calm.

“Yes, please give me much guidance from now on...”

“Do whatever.”

Baek Cheon’s head turned aside at the abrupt sarcastic voice.

Chung Myung, slumped against a wall, wore a look that seemed to say, ‘Oh my, aren’t you special.’

“That’s a pretty sight.”

“Watching the future Sect leader and the next-in-line praise each other like, ‘Oh! The Sect leader’s position is so hard! Thank you for your toil!’ makes me glad that I’ll never be a Sect leader in my life. Right?”

Baek Cheon glanced at the others as if to ask them to intervene. But even the Five Swords, usually zealous and ready to do anything for him, quietly averted their eyes at this moment.

‘Blood should be avoided, not fought.’

‘If you touch that the wrong way now, you’ll really see something awful happen.’

Even the dog at a village school can recite poems after three years—how could anyone living beside Chung Myung for years fail to read the mood?

Right now he’s basically a barrel of burning oil. No one wants to get near a barrel that’ll explode at the slightest touch.

But that’s their stance; Baek Cheon’s stance was entirely different.

‘...Bastards.’

They just said they’d help with all their might.

He are the ones who’d run without looking back if Demonic Cult members showed up. No, he’d kick Baek Cheon and throw him out as bait.

Reminded once more that there’s no one trustworthy in the world, Baek Cheon glanced toward Chung Myung and carefully spoke.

“Ahem. Um! Hey... Chung Myung...”

“Huh?”

Chung Myung’s face instantly lit up.

“Oh my. The Acting Sect leader of Mount Hua is even speaking directly to this lowly third class disciple.”

“...”

“I’m so honored I don’t know what to do.”

Baek Cheon’s face gradually grew pale.

‘I knew this man would be like this.’

When he resolved to request the Acting Sect leader post, the thing that worried him most wasn’t the weight he’d have to bear as Sect leader, but that damned man’s reaction.

Look! See how that bastard’s eyes have rolled back!

“Even if the sect goes to hell, there’s a limit. Skipping ranks and shares, some greenhouse kid takes the Sect leader seat!”

“Huh? Isn’t there supposed to be norms in a sect! Rules! Huh?”

“Chung Myung.”

“If you keep ignoring principles saying it’s just this once, the once-great sect will instantly become third-rate! In the Mount Hua of history and tradition, this...”

“Chung Myung.”

“Not until dirt gets in my eyes...”

“Chung Myung!”

“Yes?”

After babbling on, Chung Myung looked up. Baek Cheon tilted his chin up, displaying a sharp jawline, and spat out in the most obnoxious tone imaginable.

“If you’re so upset, you be the Sect leader.”

For a moment Chung Myung’s mouth hung open.

“Uh...? Uh uh...”

“Or go argue with the Sect leader.”

“...”

“You can’t, right? You can’t do anything, can you?”

“...”

“Then shh. Keep quiet, third class disciple.”

As Baek Cheon grinned, Chung Myung’s body slumped sideways.

Thunk.

His eyes lost their light like a rotten fish, and his slack mouth seemed to expel the last remnants of his soul.

“Why... why did I...”

The Five Swords, watching Chung Myung be knocked out with a single blow, genuinely marveled and looked at Baek Cheon.

“Unbelievable... Chung Myung felled by just a few words...”

“Is this the power of authority?”

“That bastard’s surprisingly weak against power.”

“‘Surprisingly’ my foot—he’s blatantly weak.”

Yoon Jong whispered to Baek Cheon.

“Wasn’t that a bit harsh?”

“No... that bastard needed that. Otherwise he’d have kept that up for another year.”

“True...”

Yoon Jong nodded in agreement.

Baek Cheon waved his hand and spoke.

“Anyway, there’s a lot to say, but let’s do what must be done first.”

“Yes? What must be done? Is something urgent now?”

“Yoon Jong, Jo Gul.”

“Yes, sasuk!”

“Go and relay my message to the sect leaders of each clan.”

A meaningful smile played at Baek Cheon’s lips.

“A man must keep his word. We’re going to save the Southern Island Sect.”

“Ah!”

Only then did Jo Gul, remembering the matter, gasp with a ‘oh no’ expression—so many shocking events had occurred one after another that he’d completely forgotten.

“We’re not in a position to finish everything before leaving. Tell them to quickly discuss the situation.”

“Understoo...”

“What? Southern Island?”

At that moment, Chung Myung, who had been nearly dead, rolled his eyes and sprang up.

“Hey, you crazy man! What on earth are you trying to do with Mount Hua?!”

“Ah, then if you’re so aggrieved you should have joined earlier and taken the Baek disciples rank. Oh dear, what to do? Even if I wanted to give you the Sect leader seat, I can’t give it to a Chung disciple rank, can I? Someone insisted the sect has rules to be observed.” |

“I-I...! I... ugh!”

When Chung Myung finally grabbed the back of his neck and fell backward, the Five Swords exchanged curious looks.

‘I like this?’

‘Actually, good?’

They were already beginning to feel the benefits of the Sect leader’s change.

“How is it?”

“I know that’s unlikely... but there’s no problem, right?”

“Is he all right?”

Tang Soso turned her sullen face. Her gaze landed on Chung Myung, who was lying on a futon, groaning in pain.

“Uh... well...”

She hesitated slightly, squeezed her eyes shut, and said.

“It’s anger sickness.”

“Anger sickness?”

“Yes, anger sickness.”

Yoon Jong blinked.

“So... uh... you mean...”

Glancing at the ailing Chung Myung, he asked awkwardly.

“Is there really such a thing as anger sickness?”

“There is. You’ve probably heard many stories of someone suffering a great trauma, wasting away, and dying.”

“...I’ve heard of that.”

“That’s anger sickness.”

Yoon Jong turned his head back to look at the lying image of Chung Myung. Sitting carefully beside the groaning man, Hae Yeon gently wetted a towel and pressed it to his forehead with tender hands.

From him being extremely careful in caring for the patient, one could tell how kind-hearted a nun Hae Yeon was.

There was, however, one minor issue...

“Maha Prajnaparamita Heart Sutra, Kuan-Yin Bodhisattva, practicing the Prajnaparamita...”

“Monk! Don’t chant sutras while caring for the patient!”

“It sounds like you’re laying out a corpse!”

“No chants! No chants!”

When the Five Swords shouted, Jo Gul, who had stepped up beside Hae Yeon intending to chant the sutras with him, flinched and slunk back. Yoon Jong clicked his tongue at Jo Gul and looked at Tang Soso.

“But anger sickness—what the hell is that...”

“It’s unusual, for sure.”

Tang Soso pursed her lips.

“Usually this sickness hits the faint-hearted or old folks full of pent-up anger, but that fellow...”

“Doesn’t fall into that category.”

“Not just one bit. That’s not a disease he should get.”

“Of all the people I know, Chung Myung is the least someone you’d call faint-hearted, sahyung.”

“Right?”

Yoon Jong clicked his tongue repeatedly as he looked at the writhing Chung Myung.

“Even if he caught anger sickness, to see a murim warrior collapse and writhe like that...”

“Ah, no, sahyung. It’s not something to say like that.”

“Huh? Why?”

Jo Gul said with a slightly pale face.

“Come to think of it, isn’t this really serious? That bastard didn’t make a sound even when he was soaked in blood at the Northern Ice Sea.”

“If that kind of man is clutching his head and lying like that, he’s really in pain.”

“...There’s sense to that.”

After hearing that, the ailing Chung Myung even looked a bit pitiful... well, actually not really pitiful... anyway!

“How much rage must have built up in him...”

“Indeed...”

The moment that was said, their gazes naturally turned to one place.

Baek Cheon, slumped against the wall, had the face of someone who’d lost everything, his head turned to the side. He even looked a little drained of color...

Feeling their gaze, Baek Cheon slowly opened his dry, cracked lips.

“I...”

A dying voice slipped through his trembling lips.

“That I rose to the position of Acting Sect leader...”

“...is something worthy of collapsing and moaning like that?”

“...”

“Huh?”

Well, who knows, sasuk. Who could say.

“So what I’m saying is... for that fellow, me becoming the Acting Sect leader is more infuriating than Jang Ilso being ready for sabotage... the Demonic Cult rising again... Southern Edge running rampant or Shaolin dancing a victory jig?”

“...Is it really like that?”

“I should die, I should... what riches or glory am I to enjoy...”

Baek Cheon began to wither away before their eyes. Yoon Jong, looking back and forth between the blank-stared Baek Cheon and the ailing Chung Myung, let out a heavy sigh.

This must be what they mean by a no-win fight.

“I’ve lived to see a warrior get anger sickness. That bastard’s something else.”

“I wouldn’t say that’s wrong.”

“Ah! You scared me!”

“Speak up when you appear, monk!”

Hae Yeon had moved up, leaving Chung Myung tossed aside, and shoved his shiny shaved head forward. When everyone jumped, he looked a bit embarrassed and said.

“...Amitabha, sorry. You were saying something interesting.”

“But you said that’s wrong?”

Hae Yeon nodded and continued.

“It’s not strange for a murim warrior to get anger sickness. In truth, by another name, murim people are the ones most prone to it in the world.”

“Eh? I don’t think I’ve ever heard of a murim warrior dying of anger sickness.”

“Change the phrase a bit and you’ll hear it often. In the Jianghu there’s a term for when accumulated anger harms the body, isn’t there?”

“Huh? O-oh, do you mean Heart Demon?”

“Yes.”

Hae Yeon smiled faintly.

“And Turning Into Demon, broadly speaking, can be the same phenomenon. It’s not rare for a mental shock to bring someone into the process of Turning into Demon.”

“I see.”

“So if you think about it, none are as prone to anger sickness as the murim people. Those living in the secular world don’t guard against anger sickness the way us murim folk watch for Heart Demon or Turning Into Demon.”

“...Come to think of it, that’s true.”

“Aren’t murim people, when you come down to it, the weakest?”

“They’re the kind most likely to get anger sickness.”

After all, the murim folk don’t care about civil affairs; if things go wrong, they draw their blades first. They are the sort who would crash in rather than resolve matters with words or petition the magistrate.

So they’re precisely the type to develop anger sickness.

Especially that one — among murim folk he’s notorious for being quick-tempered and vile. Looking back, it’s more surprising he hasn’t already fallen into Heart Demon...

“Wait a moment. Are you saying Chung Myung is now in Heart Demon?”

“Because the sasuk became the Sect leader, he went into Heart Demon?”

“Is he even human?”

As Baek Cheon’s color faded further, Tang Soso cut in.

“No. That’s just anger sickness.”

“...”

“Not Heart Demon or Turning Into Demon—pure anger sickness. If it were possession or Heart Demon, I’d know.”

“True.”

“Right. Soso wouldn’t not know.”

“It has nothing to do with martial arts. Actually, most people might have succumbed to Turning Into Demon, but sahyung’s constitution and martial base are so steady he may have resisted. In any case, it comes down to him losing his temper and collapsing on the spot.”

“...Is that something to be relieved about?”

“Is it relief?”

Yoon Jong smiled contentedly and looked at Chung Myung, clutching his head as he lay.

“I’m reminded of the saying ‘Renew yourself daily, renew yourself daily and renew yourself daily.’”

“Isn’t that a good saying?”

“It’s a good saying. I already know that fellow’s temper is excessively vile, but the more I see him day by day becoming newly more vile, how could that not be a good thing?”

“Just curse him out plainly.”

“That madman.”

At that moment, Yu Yiseol entered, glanced around, spotted the slumped Baek Cheon, and poked him with his finger.

“Sahyung.”

“Sahyung.”

“Uh, uh-huh?”

Baek Cheon, barely holding onto the tail end of his fading consciousness, lifted his head and looked at Yu Yiseol.

“Sect Leaders. Everyone’s gathered. We must go.”

“Sect leaders... the meeting, we must go. Yes. We must go...”

Baek Cheon, drained of color, wobbled like a slug as he got to his feet.

“We must go. I’m the Acting Sect leader... I can’t be late, even if I’m young. Don’t worry. I’ll go now.”

Tang Soso watched Baek Cheon move off with a start, then suddenly had a thought.

“Jo Gul sahyung.”

“Hm?”

“Let the next Sect leader simply be you, sahyung.”

“...What nonsense all of a sudden.”

“The good seat should be yours, sahyung.”

Just as Jo Gul, feeling an inexplicable unease, began to protest, it happened.

“H-hey, where? Where are you going?”

Chung Myung, who had been lying there groaning, suddenly sat bolt upright, his eyes glaring wildly.

“Sect leaders? Meeting?”

“...Why’s he like that again?”

“Soso, do you have any sleeping poison? One dose and he’d sleep for a month.”

“I have sleeping poison, but...”

“Give it. I have some use for it.”

“If misused, it’ll kill him.”

“That might be better. Think about it.”

Chung Myung kicked the blanket off his body, sprang to his feet, then staggered as if dizzy.

“I, I...”

“What’s wrong with you, brat!”

As everyone tried to stop him and asked, Chung Myung rolled his eyes.

“I’m... I’m going too!”

“No, you’re not even well...”

“No, I can’t! Who knows what that man will do! It’s Mount Hua I resurrected! I can’t let him ruin it!”

“Chung Myung... it’s the sasuk.”

“Right. I can’t let that sasuk bastard ruin things.”

No matter how you look at it, Mount Hua is you ruining it right now, Chung Myung. At this rate, you’re just going to make a bigger mess.

“I-I have to go...”

As he tried to move, Chung Myung grabbed the back of his head and staggered.

“Ugh... the back of my head...”

“He’s going to collapse.”

“Shouldn’t someone support him?”

“I know we should, but honestly I don’t want to touch him.”

“Me neither.”

The people around recoiled, but nothing like that reached Chung Myung’s eyes now.

“I, I will protect Mount Hua... Mount Hua...”

The Five Swords all sighed in unison as they watched Baek Cheon lumber out like a zombie and Chung Myung follow like a patient.

What a sect... running just fine.

“First of all...”

Tang Gunak cleared his throat and began to speak.

If Hyun Jong had been here as before, Tang Gunak wouldn’t need to say this, but Hyun Jong was not attending—perhaps to lend support to Baek Cheon.

So, acting as the de facto sect leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, he needed to organize the situation and speak.

“It’s something you all know, so it’s redundant to repeat, but Taoist Baek Cheon has been appointed as Acting Sect leader of Mount Hua.”

“Yes, family lord.”

“In that position there was so much chaos it passed awkwardly, but originally it’s something everyone should congratulate him for.”

Those gathered all nodded.

“Yes, well... with fresh young blood joining the core of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, we should offer congratulations for the future Baek Cheon as acting Sect leader will forge for Mount Hua and the Alliance... though, well...”

Tang Gunak, looking somewhat deflated, glanced at Baek Cheon and Chung Myung seated before him.

“...Acting Sect leader.”

“Yes?”

“...Why do you look like that?”

“...”

“Did you run into the Demonic Cult in that short time?”

Baek Cheon spoke sadly with his half-faded face.

“I’m... fine, so please don’t worry.”

Baek Cheon croaked like a sick chicken, but Chung Myung was in a worse state—alternating between brooding, raging, and being a mess, as if he’d lost his mind. He seemed to waver with every breath.

‘Alliance leader...’

Tang Gunak squeezed his eyes shut with a strange feeling of dread.

‘How on earth did you endure this?’

A terrible stomach-ache of responsibility that anyone handling Mount Hua must endure descended on Tang Gunak in that moment.

Maybe it’s just a stereotype, but isn’t there something like that?

The usual expectations when the leader of a group or organization changes and a younger person takes the seat.

There will of course be concerns, but if the transition is smooth, people generally expect the new blood’s vigor and broad ambitions.

But this...

Tang Gunak’s eyes trembled.

‘My elder uncle in Sichuan would be far more energetic than these fools.’

He’s old enough that his mind wanders, but surely he’s better than the sickly chicken, isn’t he?

What made his stomach turn further was that the Acting Sect leader, who was such a wreck he made that handsome face look ugly, was the very one who recently inflamed Tang Gunak’s heart in the confrontation with Beop Jeong.

‘I just can’t figure out the Mount Hua lot. I just can’t...’

Chung Myung swinging between a vicious Sword Master and a crazed brute, or Baek Cheon alternating between the revered next Acting Sect leader expected by Mount Hua and the village idiot brother Dong-Ryong...

The thought that Tang Gunak would have to discuss the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s affairs with such people made Hyun Jong’s absence painfully keen.

“So... um...”

Tang Gunak, who couldn’t stay silent forever, opened his mouth reluctantly.

“They say you built this place?”

“Yes?”

“I mean this position?”

“...Me?”

“......”

Then Tang Gunak realized why violence was so rampant at Mount Hua.

When people like this live together, wouldn’t fists naturally come before words?

“Ah... ah, right. I... yes, I did.”

“......”

“So, the reason I gathered you all is...”

Baek Cheon tilted his head.

“...What was it again?”

And at that moment Tang Gunak saw it.

Chung Myung’s face, which had been swinging between anger and gloom, twisted as if possessed by an evil, hideous malice.

Tang Gunak could swear he’d never seen a sight so terrifying in his life. Even if the Heavenly Demon descended before him, he wouldn’t feel this fear.

“...There was a reason.”

“Hey, you!”

Just as Chung Myung’s eyes rolled back and he began convulsing, the Five Swords, who had been waiting, leapt forward, clamped his mouth shut and pinned him down.

Even Hae Yeon, who usually clicked his tongue from the back, was startled and pressed Chung Myung’s chest with his shaved head, sprawled over him.

“Chung Myung! He’s the Acting Sect leader, Acting Sect leader!”

“Other people are watching!”

“Mouth! Shut your mouth, mouth! Say the wrong thing here and you’ll really be dragged off to the repentance hall!”

“Hold him properly there!”

Watching that scene in bewilderment, Tang Gunak unconsciously covered his face with both hands and hung his head.

‘Is this really okay?’

Wouldn’t it be better to join hands with the Nine Great Sects now? Even those clans that fiercely opposed them would, seeing this with their own eyes, think, “Isn’t Shaolin a prestigious school with history and tradition?” and maybe run to Mount Song without looking back.

At that moment Yu Yiseol rapped Baek Cheon’s dazed head and handed him a bottle of cold water.

“Drink.”

“...Ah.”

Baek Cheon drank the cold water in one gulp and his face gradually regained color.

It was a curious sight. He merely tightened an unruly expression that hadn’t been worth much, yet that alone made him look like a different person.

“I’m sorry. I’m not in the best condition.”

“When have you ever been?”

“Yes?”

“...Nothing.”

Tang Gunak bit his lip. Seems that damned Mount Hua’s way of speaking has rubbed off on him. After he said that.

Baek Cheon cleared his throat softly and spoke.

“The reason I invited the various sect leaders is to discuss what the Heavenly Friends Alliance should prioritize most.”

“Discuss...”

Muttering it quietly, Tang Gunak nodded slightly and said:

“I hope you mean precisely, Acting Sect leader. Do you mean to decide what should be done first? Or to discuss methods for something you’ve already decided?”

“The latter, more or less.”

“Hmm.”

Tang Gunak’s expression became slightly awkward.

Hyun Jong would not have spoken this way. He listened to others’ opinions before deciding. But Baek Cheon wants to discuss something he’s already decided.

It could be brushed off as a difference in temperament, but it felt a bit uncomfortable.

Then Baek Cheon spoke.

“Ah, please don’t misunderstand.”

“Hmm?”

Noticing Tang Gunak’s expression, he calmly elaborated.

“I am merely Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader, not the deputy Alliance Leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. What I will say is not to act in place of the Alliance Leader, but simply to present Mount Hua’s opinion as its Acting Sect leader.”

“...Hmm.”

“The authority to decide has always rested with the Alliance Leader, so please don’t assume that what I say is the Alliance Leader’s opinion.”

It meant this was a proposal from below, not an order from above. Strictly speaking, Mount Hua’s acting Sect leader’s actual status is lower than the Tang family, which holds the deputy alliance leader role, and is no different from other sects.

However...

“Acting Sect leader. Given the circumstances, it’s hard for us to think that way. First, the fact that the Alliance Leader and you belong to the same sect...”

“Would the leader favor our opinion?”

“...He will understand.”

Tang Gunak nodded immediately.

He couldn’t be sure of any other place. But Hyun Jong would never do that; his fairness was beyond doubt.

Moreover, Hyun Jong ridiculously trusted his disciples and at the same time...

‘...was also the person who least trusted his own disciples in the world.’

More precisely, he perhaps didn’t distrust them so much as understood their fanaticism better than anyone... anyway.

So there would be no special deference to Mount Hua’s opinion.

“But if that’s the case, Mount Hua will be treated as just another sect with equal voice. Is that acceptable?”

“Isn’t that only natural?”

Baek Cheon answered at once as if with no hesitation.

“It should have been that way. That’s what the Heavenly Friends Alliance is. The reason Mount Hua appeared to lead the alliance was simply because the Alliance Leader also held Mount Hua’s sect’s leader position. If the lord of the Tang family becomes the leader, the Tang family will assume that role.”

“Hmm.”

“Mount Hua has no intention of demanding privileges within the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Our founding principle is equality for all, isn’t it?”

Tang Gunak’s mouth twitched into a small smile without him noticing.

‘Indeed...’

People are called indifferent to power when they aren’t clinging to potential power. In the end, no matter how indifferent someone seems, letting go of power they already enjoy isn’t easy.

Power is comfort.

Why refuse the comfort given and choose discomfort? Especially if no one will blame you, there’s even less reason to do so.

But now Baek Cheon says Mount Hua will calmly relinquish even the implicit superior rights and position it held in the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Maybe that’s possible because he’s young, but even so, it was impressive.

“Is that Mount Hua’s stance?”

“Yes, lord of the Tang family.”

“Hmm. That’s good. Young people are certainly different.”

Tang Gunak nodded, then suddenly made a strange face and said:

“But there’s something.”

“Yes.”

“...It seems there’s someone who thinks differently from you?”

“Yes?”

At that, Baek Cheon glanced to his side.

“Ugh! Ugh!”

Chung Myung, pinned by the Five Swords, writhed with bloodshot eyes. Those eyes full of hatred and malice seemed to shout, “How did you earn that right, and you just give it away as you please! You damned son of a bitch! Get out of Mount Hua!”

“Ughhhh!”

“Just stay still!”

“Someone go get a rope, quickly!”

“A... a hemp rope?”

“Not hemp rope, a steel cable! What are you going to do tying this bastard with hemp!”

“No, where are we even supposed to get a steel cable right now...”

The Five Swords pressed down desperately on the struggling Chung Myung. Watching that strange, terrifying spectacle, a faint, sardonic smile crossed Baek Cheon’s lips.

“Lord of the Tang family.”

“Hmm?”

Baek Cheon lifted his gaze briskly and, looking at Tang Gunak, spoke very calmly:

“You needn’t worry about it.”

“...R-really?”

“Yes. I speak now as Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader. I don’t understand why you’d concern yourself with the reaction of a mere third class disciple.”

“Cough, cough.”

Tang Gunak suddenly choked and coughed violently.

“A mere third class disciple?”

“Don’t mind him. He’s just one of Mount Hua’s countless third class disciples. We invited him because he can actually use a sword, but at most he’s just a third class disciple and completely irrelevant to Mount Hua’s official stance—so treat him as if he isn’t here.”

“Uh...”

Which would carry more weight: my words representing Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader, or that ‘mere’ third class disciple’s?

“Well, th-that... no, it could... it should be...”

“Ha. Isn’t that painfully obvious?”

Tang Gunak watched as Baek Cheon gently asked, glanced at Chung Myung, and gave a small smile.

Seeing that face, Chung Myung began trembling convulsively, then soon foamed white at the mouth.

“Gurgle...”

Chung Myung made a strange noise, rolled his eyes and collapsed, and the room fell silent. The spasms twitching intermittently looked like he’d drifted very far away.

‘Is this... really okay?’

Of course it’s correct in principle. No matter how skilled the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is, as a matter of status he’s only a third class disciple of Mount Hua. By protocol and principle, he shouldn’t have a speaking role at such a meeting.

‘But still—he’s Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?’

Is this really allowed?

“Not as a mere third class disciple, but as Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader, I’ll say this...”

“Hmm?”

“What the Heavenly Friends Alliance must prioritize now is to carry out a consensus and make it clear that we are different from the Nine Great Sects.”

“Th-that makes sense.”

“Therefore Mount Hua proposes to the other sect leaders that they immediately muster personnel and depart for Southern Island. Prepare as quickly as possible—no more than three days—and aim to push through Southern lands and reach Southern Island.”

“Um... that’s a bit sudden...”

“Only those confident in their martial prowess should join. If no one joins, Mount Hua will head to Southern Island alone.”

Silence followed. Baek Cheon finished and grinned.

“That’s all.”

At that moment Tang Gunak realized: this mad Acting Sect leader wasn’t only attacking Beop Jeong.

Tang Gunak’s stomach began to churn bitterly again.

“W-well... no, Acting Sect leader.”

Tang Gunak, speaking in a slightly flustered voice, gave a loud cough. He then barely regained a composed expression and sighed.

“Of course, I agree with the Acting Sect leader’s argument that we must go to Southern Island.”

There is no room for disagreement here.

The Heavenly Friends Alliance had already resolved to take a different path from the Nine Great Sects. At the heart of that decision was clearly a journey to the Southern Island Sect. If they now hesitated to head to Southern Island, whatever they had said at the negotiation with Beop Jeong would be dismissed as mere lip service.

So up to this point, it’s only natural.

“And I know that Southern Island’s situation isn’t a simple one to take up. If possible, we should hurry. But hurrying and being reckless are by no means the same thing.”

Baek Cheon also nodded, as if agreeing with that remark.

“Going to Southern Island is not to be taken lightly. We must reach the deep heart of Southern lands. Naturally, sufficient preparation and precautions are necessary. If we act hastily, we might ruin everything, won’t we?”

“I fully agree with Master.”

Baek Cheon nodded and looked at Tang Gunak.

“We must prepare thoroughly, whether to reduce sacrifices or to minimize damage.”

“But why...”

“Precisely because of that.”

“Hm?”

For a moment Tang Gunak tilted his head, not understanding Baek Cheon’s meaning. Baek Cheon then calmly elaborated.

“Now, hurrying as much as possible is the way to reduce harm.”

“...Do you mean to Southern Island?”

“No.”

Baek Cheon answered immediately without a moment’s hesitation.

“Of course, shortening the time will help the Southern Island well. But the reason we must truly hurry is not because of the Southern Island sect, it’s because of those who must go to Southern Lands.”

“...Explain that in more detail.”

“Our discussions about going to the Southern Islands have not been just for a day or two. Even if only a few within the Alliance spoke, words inevitably leak out, don’t they?”

Tang Gunak nodded as if acknowledging the logic of that statement.

“And recently even the Abbot learned of our intentions. The Abbot will have to explain to others why the negotiations failed, so it’s highly likely that this matter will be discussed.”

Tang Gunak finally nodded as if he now understood.

“You mean our plan to go to Southern Islands could become known to the Four Sects Alliance.”

“Yes, that’s right. It’s not that I don’t trust those I spoke with. But words are like water; you can’t keep them contained forever. Even without ill intent. In fact, because there’s no ill intent, they leak out more easily.”

Tang Gunak’s face grew slightly serious. He thought the point made sense.

‘The Four Sects Alliance...’

If Shaolin hadn’t intervened things might have been different, but now the likelihood that their trip to Southern Island Sect could no longer be hidden had increased.

“This just got awkward.”

From the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s point of view, Southern land is essentially hostile territory. And if the Four Sects Alliance learns of their journey to their home ground?

‘What was a scattered ground will become a deadly ground.’

Tang Gunak, his face hardened, looked at Baek Cheon.

“Acting Sect leader.”

“Yes, Family lord.”

“I understand the Acting Sect leader’s words. But it’s true I have concerns. I can see why you’d say we must hurry because the situation could worsen, but...”

“You mean it could look like rushing into a dangerous place unprepared.”

“Indeed.”

As Tang Gunak conceded, Baek Cheon opened his mouth again.

“I have considered that as well. But this time, dragging our feet won’t produce a solution. While we deliberate, they will be devising countermeasures.”

“Hmm. But...”

“And the one who will devise those countermeasures is Jang Ilso.”

Tang Gunak fell silent upon hearing that.

Prepare strategies and meet each other in opposition? Everyone here knows what that outcome would be. On a battlefield where both sides are prepared, there is no one in the current murim world who can stand up to Jang Ilso.

“Of course I know. It would seem hasty and dangerous.”

Tang Gunak did not deny it; he merely listened to Baek Cheon.

“But until now the Heavenly Friends Alliance has spent far too much time reconciling opinions and reaching consensus to find the best course. That method may have been right so far, but from now on it will not be.”

Tang Gunak’s expression stiffened slightly.

He understood what ‘from now on’ meant. When the specter of war covers the murim world, the leisurely method of everyone gathering to gather opinions and find an answer becomes useless. What matters then are swift decisions and bold execution.

“Is that your view?”

“Yes, family lord.”

Baek Cheon nodded.

“But if that choice is wrong, it will produce irreversible consequences.”

“That’s why I suggested three days. I cannot find the best method alone. But with those present here and three days to think, we may not find the best but we can find a reasonable second-best.”

“......”

“I think it’s necessary to appropriately compromise between the two approaches. We must avoid dogmatism, but we can’t stall for too long. From now on we must desperately find an answer within a limited time.”

Tang Gunak lightly stroked his chin and fell into thought. At first hearing he had thought it rash without a plan, but on hearing the explanation it was a very reasonable proposal.

He looked at Baek Cheon with renewed eyes.

Even when he boldly presented his opinion before Beop Jeong he had seemed extraordinary, but this man was certainly not ordinary. While others couldn’t move past the shocking event of Mount Hua’s new Acting Sect leader appointment, Baek Cheon was clearly already thinking about the next move.

He may be rash and immature himself, but wasn’t it said that Mount Hua’s Sect leader cannot be such?’

Seeing him now, the meaning of what Baek Cheon had said earlier became clear.

“First...”

Tang Gunak frowned slightly and said.

“Just to be sure, let me confirm—this is Mount Hua proposing a suggestion in the name of sects affiliated with the Heavenly Friends Alliance, correct?”

“Of course. If there’s a better idea, we can change our position at any time.”

“Understood.”

After touching on the necessary points, Tang Gunak asked again.

“You asked us to supply personnel—how many are you thinking? About how many per sect...?”

“As I see it, one per sect, and the total should not exceed ten.”

Tang Gunak’s eyes widened in momentary astonishment.

“You mean to go to Southern Island with barely that many people?”

“Can we confront the Four Sects Alliance with manpower like that?”

Baek Cheon shrugged his shoulders.

“Even if a hundred or two hundred went, if they are surrounded by the Four Sects Alliance in the Southern lands they wouldn’t be able to fight back effectively. And if we mobilize enough troops to take on the Four Sects Alliance?”

“...A war would begin.”

“Yes, exactly.”

Baek Cheon stared at Tang Gunak with a resolute gaze.

“Therefore, the number heading to the Southern Island must be minimized.”

“But then they won’t have any resistance if discovered by them, will they?”

“That’s why they’d be limited to those who are confident in martial skill. And if possible, each sect should select people excellent in evasive techniques.”

“...Evasive techniques?”

“Yes.”

Baek Cheon spoke proudly, his face almost radiant.

“Because if they’re discovered, they must flee.”

“...”

“When fleeing, scattering in all directions raises the survival rate, so they must be people who can escape the Four Sects Alliance’s pursuit even alone. This is the top criterion.”

“Uh...”

It made sense. Ah, of course it made sense, but...

Tang Gunak ultimately failed to maintain his composure. He stared blankly at Baek Cheon and thought.

‘Has anyone who rose to the position of Sect leader ever uttered such words?’

Moreover, it’s normal for people to watch their words once they reach a certain high position...

Tang Gunak cleared his throat and nodded.

“So Mount Hua’s proposal is to prioritize quickly reaching Southern Island with a small number skilled in evasive techniques.”

“Yes.”

Tang Gunak stroked his chin and said.

“It seems like a good idea, but it also appears to lack a plan. First, if they reach the Southern Island, isn’t the primary problem that there’s no proper way to evacuate them safely?”

“That’s not something we need to worry about.”

“Huh? What do you mean by that?”

At that moment, the corner of Baek Cheon’s mouth twisted oddly.

“By the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s usual methods, we’d perfectly plan even how to evacuate Southern island, but isn’t that actually meaningless?”

“Why is that?”

“What if we prepare all that and arrive in Southern Island, and they say they don’t trust our method and choose to stay there—what can we do then?”

“...Huh?”

Tang Gunak looked slightly stunned. Baek Cheon straightened his torso and spoke.

“The most important thing right now is to confirm the Southern Island’s intentions. What comes after can be discussed afterward. All of the current problems stem from communications with Southern Island being cut off after the Four Sects Alliance seized the Southern lands.”

“...The communication network, huh.”

“Yes. I think we should focus first on restoring that communication network.”

“Hmm. If that’s the case, then certainly it’s somewhat...”

Tang Gunak, who was about to nod, suddenly paused. Something felt odd when he thought it over.

“No... Isn’t there a contradiction in your words? If they declare they will stay in Southern Island, what meaning does it have for us to build a communication network with them?”

A strange smile spread across Baek Cheon’s face at that question.

“We would have no meaning in that case, of course.”

“But?”

“Family lord. It’s not always necessary that deeds be meaningful. Sometimes the value lies in doing good out of compassion, isn’t that so?”

“Compassion?”

“Think about it.”

Baek Cheon said, sounding genuinely pained.

“How tormented would others be to see a brotherly sect from the same grouping isolated in enemy territory, cut off from proper communication?”

“One grouping? The Nine... Nine Great Sects?”

“Yes!”

A look of genuine compassion shadowed Baek Cheon’s face.

“If the Southern Island sect declared they’d accept a formal help, of course the Heavenly Friends Alliance proper would step in to help them. But even if they refuse, to just leave them there and turn our backs is not what people should do. At the very least, since we’re going to the Southern lands, we should restore the severed communications.”

“Have Shaolin and the Nine Great Sects personally receive the letter from the isolated Southern Island sect, so everyone in today’s murim world can know their situation. Isn’t that human decency and the duty of Taoists?”

“...Is that normally called duty?”

“Haha, Family lord.”

Baek Cheon smiled brightly.

“This is now Mount Hua’s way.”

Tang Gunak’s face grew openly displeased. Of course, as someone who is not a Taoist, it was odd for him to debate duty and decency in front of Mount Hua, but something seemed off about the direction this was taking...

At that moment Tang Gunak’s gaze naturally turned toward one person. From the atmosphere alone, there was someone who would likely pick apart whatever Baek Cheon did. He was surely...

“Oh?”

And Tang Gunak saw it. Chung Myung, who had been glaring white-eyed a moment before, was now watching Baek Cheon with eyes bright and admiring.

“Not bad at all.”

“Heh heh, right?”

“If it goes well, even if it fails, we could paint the big bald head red.”

“This is the dignity of a Sect leader, my third class disciple.”

“Ugh... This time I admit it.”

The tension in Tang Gunak’s eyes gradually drained away.

‘Is this really okay?’

He thought quietly. Once this meeting ends he must somehow get a private audience with Hyun Jong.

Only after barely sorting out the whirlwind of chaotic thoughts did Tang Gunak speak again.

“It’s complicated, but to put it simply: send only a small force to Southern Island first and then ascertain their intentions?”

“Yes, family lord.”

“If they say they’ll join the Heavenly Friends Alliance, then the Alliance’s main body will provide support and find a way to extract the Southern Island Sect; if they choose to stay within the Nine Great Sects, we’ll establish a line of communication between the Nine Great Sects and the Southern Island Sect?”

“Yes.”

“If it goes well, all the better; if it doesn’t, we might incidentally screw over Shaolin... no, open a path for Southern Island?”

“Exactly.”

Chung Myung grinned at that.

“In that case, I’d rather Southern Island show some loyalty. Heehee.”

“...Chung Myung. You’re a Taoist.”

“Who says I’m not? Uhi hi hi hit!”

“...”

Tang Gunak, watching Chung Myung with a wry look, rubbed his chin as if to collect his thoughts.

‘Not bad.’

Just the fact that the risk drops considerably is tempting. Honestly, it’s true—any assessment about Southern Island’s stance is nothing more than the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s speculation, right?

If we muster all our strength and march to Southern Island only for the Southern Island’s Sect leader to say, “Why have you come?”, the Heavenly Friends Alliance will be made to look foolish before the world.

‘The more troops we lead, the more likely Four Sects Alliance will take interest; exponentially so.’

So proposing a small force, easy to withdraw in an emergency, to go to Southern Island was certainly a good idea.

Having suddenly become the Acting Sect leader and said radical things in talks with Beop Jeong raised concerns, but Baek Cheon seemed to be maintaining balance better than expected.

“But.”

“Yes.”

“But how exactly do you intend to maintain that line of communication?”

Tang Gunak tilted his head as if he couldn’t grasp it.

“The Nine Great Sects weren’t trying to sever ties with the Southern Island for nothing. They had no choice. From Southern Island to the northern banks stretches at least 300km, doesn’t it?”

“Mm. That’s right.”

“Even the Beggars’ Union’s famed special courier pigeon, the Thousand meters Blue Pigeon, can’t go more than a hundred km at once. They say a thousand meters, but in truth it barely reaches 500 meters. That’s why the Beggars’ Union sets up relay stations everywhere and uses a courier relay system, isn’t it?”

Everyone nodded. This was a method well known throughout the martial world.

“But we can’t establish a branch in the southern lands, so according to your plan we’d have to create a communication line covering the roughly 800km distance without branches, is that it?”

“...”

Tang Gunak narrowed his eyes as Baek Cheon remained silent.

“You’re not seriously suggesting bringing a single letter written by the Southern Island’s Sect leader and trying to sway public opinion with it, are you? That’s not practically feasible.”

“I never said we’d create public opinion, but...”

“Hm? You were definitely going to sway public opinion to... Shaolin...”

“I said I’d merely report the situation. Aren’t you getting ahead of yourself...? Of course, I understand the family lord’s ill will toward Shaolin.”

“...”

For a moment Tang Gunak’s face flushed slightly.

“Ahem. I seem to have misunderstood for a moment.”

“A misunderstanding?”

“Just say it as that.”

“Yep.”

Tang Gunak cleared his throat again and looked at Baek Cheon.

“So? You don’t intend to sway public opinion, meaning such measures aren’t necessary?”

“Well...”

Just as Baek Cheon was about to answer, Chung Myung spoke nonchalantly.

“Why worry about that?”

“Huh?”

As Tang Gunak turned to Chung Myung, Chung Myung whipped his head around.

“Hey! Map!”

“Yes.”

At Chung Myung’s shout, Im So-Byeong immediately responded. People glanced around as if a commotion had come, and Im So-Byeong got up and spread open the map that had been lying nearby.

“Listen up!”

“...Yes.”

Everyone couldn’t bear to look at the sight and turned their heads.

Good heavens... a man who uses the Green Forest King of the realm as a map hanger.

No matter that the Greenwood King is of the Evil Sect, he doesn’t deserve such treatment... what on earth is that fellow doing attached to the Heavenly Friends Alliance?

“Look over there.”

“Hm?”

Those who saw the map automatically frowned. They already knew, but seeing it made the distance from the Southern Island to here hit them anew.

“But Southern Island is really large, isn’t it?”

“It’s about the size of a decent city? Maybe half of Shaanxi.”

“Eh, not that much as half.”

“No, not that!”

“Huh?”

Chung Myung gestured with his chin to point at the Southern Island section on the map.

“Here. Here.”

Gathering everyone’s attention, Chung Myung began to explain.

“The Southern Island does float in the middle of the sea, true, but its distance to the mainland is both far and near. There’s a peninsula just above it.”

“That’s true...”

On the large continent there’s a peninsula jutting out like a tail, and right beneath that lies Southern Island.

“So if you measure the straight-line distance between the closest shores, it’s only about sixty li.”

“Is 20km close? Even by boat it would take several hours.”

“Ah, that’s nothing.”

“...”

They felt awed by the vastness, but that was a secondary issue now.

“But... how does that relate to the communication line? You’re not suggesting sending people to cross that strait; the current problem is that people can’t enter the southern lands, right?”

“So it’s been solved.”

“Huh?”

“There are three problems in maintaining a communication line: only people can reliably travel such a huge roundtrip; people can’t enter the southern lands; and you have to cross the sea.”

“...Right?”

There are minor issues, but that’s the core of it.

Growing needlessly uneasy, Tang Gunak added cautiously.

“The Four Sects Alliance won’t be fools; they’ll be guarding that peninsula like a fortress, won’t they?”

“Yes, yes. I know.”

Chung Myung nodded as if it were obvious.

“Then use a method that solves all three at once: can do the 800km roundtrip, can cross the roughly 20 km sea in an instant, and travel there without being noticed by people!”

“Huh?”

Everyone tilted their heads. Such a person...

“The Ghost Clan?”

“No way. Even a Ghost Clan can’t do that. Express delivery still depends on circumstances.”

“...Then?”

It was at that moment.

Chung Myung suddenly shoved his hand into his front sash and pulled out something white.

“Oh?”

“Wow...”

“Good heavens.”

“Here it is... no, this bastard held on?”

“Kiiiieeeee!”

Baek Ah clung to Chung Myung’s clothes and resisted desperately. Being an animal that understood human speech, she sensed the oddity of the situation.

“Heave-ho!”

But in the end, Baek Ah was hauled out and plopped onto the floor.

Flat on the floor, Baek Ah quickly scanned around and, at the moment she tried to dash off to one side at full speed,

“Try running.”

“...”

Baek Ah’s feet froze in midair.

“Where do you think you’re running to? Right. Winter’s coming, and a scarf would be perfect—now you’ll fatten up nicely and we’ll get a thick scarf out of you.”

“...”

“You human and beast scum! If I fed and raised you, you owe your keep! That bastard from the penniless Tao-repository ate every piece of meat he could find and now tries to back out!”

“Kiiieeee...”

At that moment, people saw.

Tears like chicken droppings began to fall from the white sable’s eyes.

‘How pitiful.’

‘What happened to him to end up like this.’

‘Still, isn’t he better off than the Green Forest King?’

Meng So laughed and said upon seeing that.

“If it’s White Lightning, he could probably come and go to Southern Island without issue.”

“Th-that really works?”

“Why call her ‘White Lightning’, ‘White Lightning’ for nothing? If she sets her mind to it, she could do 350km in a day.”

“Ah, but there’s the sea?”

“Animals generally swim better than humans. That creature could cross sixty li in an instant.”

“Wow...”

“No wonder she’s a spirit beast.”

Meng So smirked upon hearing that.

“As a spirit beast specially handled even by the Beast Palace, her ability is certain. The issue until now wasn’t whether she could do it, but that we had no way to make her work...”

Meng So glanced at Chung Myung.

“...I kind of think I should learn to use him.”

“No, Palace Lord!”

“Don’t learn it!”

“The Beast Palace is supposed to be friends with animals! That’s not a friend—it’s a wicked slave market!”

Tang Gunak nodded as well.

“Indeed... they may be wary of people, but they won’t be wary of a sable. Even if they were, it’s impossible to find this small sable in that vast area.”

“See?”

“It’s rational. Truly, spirit beasts have many uses.”

“Hehehe. I fed and housed him all this time knowing this would come in handy.”

“Excellent.”

“But how about feeding and sleeping? This isn’t a day or two—no matter how fast, a roundtrip would take at least three or four days.”

“What’s there to worry about? It’s an animal. If it’s hungry, it’ll hunt; if sleepy, it’ll dig a den and sleep.”

“...They said spirit beasts are no different from humans?”

“Ah, that’s just words. When needed she’s a spirit beast; when it’s a bother she’s just an animal. Isn’t that how things go?”

“Rational.”

A grim scene unfolded: the powerful figures of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, famed across the murim world as a coalition, smiling in satisfaction as they devised a plan to exploit a tiny animal.

Baek Cheon saw Baek Ah flop on the floor and sigh deeply. It seemed as if he could hear a voice whisper, “Ha... life.”

He felt a strange kinship. Perhaps because they shared the Baek...

“Ahem. Anyway, that solves the communication network part.”

Tang Gunak hurriedly summarized the situation.

“I’ve talked long enough; the important matters are whether this plan is realistic and what the other sects think.”

Tang Gunak raised his head and looked at the other sect leaders.

“What do you think? Do you support this plan?”

No sooner had he finished than Namgung Do-Wei spoke up.

“Taoist Baek Cheon. No, Acting Sect leader.”

“Yes, Young lord.”

He looked straight at Baek Cheon as he spoke.

“Let me confirm one thing. What are the criteria for those who must head to the Southern Island?”

“As I said, those with high martial skill from each sect, especially those quick in footwork methods, would be ideal.”

Namgung Do-Wei shook his head.

“That’s not what I meant. I’m asking why you insist on taking one person from each sect.”

“Ah, that’s simple. It’s to show that all sects affiliated with the Heavenly Friends Alliance are willing to stand up for the Southern Island.”

“Understood. Then the more reputable or famous the person going to the Southern Island, the better.”

“That may be so, but...”

“Then.”

Namgung Do-Wei turned his head to look at Tang Gunak.

“The Namgung Family will send the most suitable person.”

“Huh?”

“Someone from Namgung with the highest martial prowess, great renown, and who can help persuade them...”

“C-Could it be...”

“Yes! I’ll go personally.”

Tang Gunak covered his face with both hands.

Why is it always like this...

Why are they all like this.

You miserable lot...

“Hey... Young family lord.”

Tang Gunak sighed deeply and spoke.

“The journey to Southern Island is far more dangerous than you think. Even if the goal is to go unnoticed with only a few people, it is still risky.”

“I know, Family Lord.”

“And yet, the Namgung Family’s young lord... you, who in practice fulfill the role of the family lord, intend to go to the Southern Island yourself?”

Tang Gunak raised his voice a little as if it were absurd.

“You aren’t suggesting this without realizing what would happen if you too were harmed in the current state of the Namgung Family, are you?”

It was a cold reprimand, but Namgung Do-Wei merely smiled calmly.

“Of course I know.”

“And yet you still plan to go to the Southern Island?”

“Yes.”

Hearing that resolute answer without a trace of hesitation, Tang Gunak shut his eyes tightly.

‘Namgung Hwang would’ve been better to deal with.’

Namgung Hwang also had his reckless side, but that stemmed from pride that burst out when he couldn’t master his own feelings.

But this man, unlike Namgung Hwang, had thought everything through and withheld emotion, yet arrived at the same conclusion as Namgung Hwang.

If the process is different but the result is the same, isn’t it easier to deal with the one whose process is wrong?

“Young lord...”

“Acting Sect leader.”

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei turned his head and looked at Baek Cheon.

“Yes, young lord.”

“What do you intend to do, Acting Sect leader? Having risen to the Acting Sect leader of Mount Hua, will you stay in the rear and send your disciples to the Southern Island?”

At that, Baek Cheon shrugged his shoulders.

“There is no such Acting Sect leader in Mount Hua. Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader is the one who acts first at the forefront. Of course I will go to the Southern Island myself.”

Hearing that, Namgung Do-Wei looked at Tang Gunak as if to say ‘see?’.

“That’s right.”

“...”

“If Mount Hua’s Acting Sect leader goes to the Southern Island in person, why would the Namgung Family’s young lord withdraw because of some lofty title? The Namgung Family cannot match Mount Hua in strength, but at least we will not lose in united resolve.”

“No. I hope you don’t feel competitive over such needless matters...”

“That’s not all. As you said, Lord, the Namgung Family is not whole right now. Most of those who once represented the Namgung died on Plum Blossom Island. Because of that, there are few left who can represent the Namgung by name alone.”

“...”

“Therefore.”

Namgung Do-Wei relaxed his shoulders and began to speak.

“One who is a direct victim of what the Nine Great Sects did, who has experienced how brutal Four Sects Alliance can be, a member of the Namgung Family who can represent its position, and someone who can harmonize well with those heading to Southern Island and successfully lead every mission.”

“...”

“There is no one more suitable in the Namgung than myself.”

Tang Gunak closed his mouth like a clamshell.

He tried to find a retort, but no rebuttal came to mind—the argument was almost flawless logically.

So Tang Gunak had no choice but to utter words uncharacteristic of his usual self.

“Listen, young lord.”

“Yes, Family Lord.”

“As you know, your father and I were on friendly terms. We weren’t intimate friends, but there was no problem sharing a drink together.”

“I know. If circumstances had been different, the two of you might have been closer.”

“Yes. That’s true.”

Tang Gunak let out a deep sigh.

“Then you should consider my position a little as well. If you were harmed on this trip to Southern Island, how could I face that friend?”

“You needn’t worry about that.”

Namgung Do-Wei smiled softly.

“The father I know wouldn’t blame me for going to Southern Island. He would rather blame me for not going. My father was that kind of man.”

“...”

Tang Gunak was struck dumb.

After thinking, he realized Namgung Hwang truly was that kind of person.

“Well, you have an uncle too, don’t you!”

“My uncle’s wounds from before are too deep for him to endure a long journey. As his nephew, how could I ask an injured uncle to undertake such a thing?”

“Th-that...”

If Namgung Myung had been here he would have immediately retorted, ‘What? I’m perfectly fine,’ but unfortunately Namgung Myung was not present.

“Th-then...”

Just as Tang Gunak was trying to squeeze out a reply, Meng So snorted and spoke.

“Family Lord, your position must be awkward as well.”

At that remark Tang Gunak couldn’t help but sigh deeply.

In truth, Tang Gunak was not one to stop others or hold people back with excuses; he was typically indifferent and watched events unfold with calm detachment.

But now that he was presiding over a Heavenly Friends Alliance meeting, he realized that behaving as he normally did might send them all to hell in less than three days.

‘Alliance leader...’

Tang Gunak felt anew, with bitter clarity, what Hyun Jong had to do as the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Sometimes he had been frustrated, wondering why Hyun Jong always nitpicked when the youngsters would inevitably get their way...

‘If you don’t object, these kids will go as far as the Western Regions.’

So even if it didn’t change the outcome, raising objections had its own value.

“Ugh. You have no intention of changing your mind... of course not. Fine, I understand.”

Tang Gunak sighed deeply and opened his mouth again.

“No. But my original question was whether you agree with Mount Hua’s position...”

“I, too, from the Ice Palace will go in person!”

“...”

Tang Gunak turned his head with a soul-dead gaze. He saw Seol So-Baek’s flushed face.

“O-of course the Ice Palace has many greater and more distinguished people than me. But the Ice Palace is not well known in the Central Plains, so whoever goes would have difficulty making a strong impression.”

“...No, Palace Lord, first of all...”

“Therefore I think it’s right that I of the Ice Palace go personally. Even if I’m weak, I hold the position of the palace’s Lord; even if we lack people, the authority of the position carries persuasive power!”

For a moment, faces of Ice Palace elders who looked at Seol So-Baek kindly came to Tang Gunak’s mind.

‘I don’t particularly disagree with that statement...’

Elders.

If you intended that, you shouldn’t have brought that child here... or at least you should have kept him away from that water-sprinkled ink-like fellow... (implying Chung Myung).

Tang Gunak’s gaze turned to Chung Myung, who was looking at Seol So-Baek with a pleased ‘ha, that one’s grown well’ expression.

That man is the source of all evil. All evil...

“Palace Lord... I asked whether you supported Mount Hua’s opinion...”

“I agree! Of course I agree! I have no objections.”

“No. Don’t be so hasty...”

“The Ice Palace rose again from isolation with Mount Hua’s help. Had Mount Hua not come to the Northern Sea then, would we be here now?”

“...”

Tang Gunak quickly shut his mouth.

“A person must know duty to be a person. The Ice Palace that rose again cannot turn its back on those in the same predicament.”

“Y-yes. Of course.”

If someone else had said this, Tang Gunak would have retorted ‘life is not like that in reality,’ but in front of Seol So-Baek he couldn’t bring himself to say it.

Telling that child such a thing would feel like kicking away a growing sprout, a sinful act.

Tang Gunak felt painfully that youth could sometimes be a weapon.

“That’s a good idea.”

“Palace Lord!”

Meng So said awkwardly.

“But you have thought about how to persuade the elders, right?”

“Ah... that is...”

When Seol So-Baek looked flustered, Meng So chuckled.

“As a fellow Palace Lord, I’ll give you one piece of advice.”

“Yes! I will listen.”

“Sometimes you must understand and use the fact that you are the Palace Lord. If something must be done, getting formal agreement is not always important.”

“Ah...”

“When this meeting ends, come to my quarters, I’ll show you what a Palace Lord’s ‘persuasion’ looks like.”

“Thank you.”

“What are you teaching that kid...”

“If he’s the Lord of the First Palace, he can no longer be called a child. Isn’t that so?”

Tang Gunak’s eyes wavered.

If he said otherwise here, wouldn’t that be an insult to the Ice Palace’s Lord? He couldn’t possibly say that.

Whether aware of Tang Gunak’s feelings or not, Seol So-Baek, with a flushed face, asked Meng So.

“Then, Palace Lord, will you go yourself?”

“The Beast Palace will not participate this time.”

“W-why is that?”

“Because we are foreigners.”

Meng So snorted with laughter.

“The Heavenly Friends Alliance may not see it that way, but people of the Central Plains still hold prejudices against outsiders. If two such outsiders go to persuade them, it won’t leave a very good impression.”

“...But isn’t the Southern Island sect also in a remote place?”

“That’s why I said it’s okay for the Ice Palace to go. But it’s not good for both sects to go. This is a decision for the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“Hmm.”

Seol So-Baek nodded with a serious face.

“Besides, hasn’t the Beast Palace already chosen its representative? And it’s someone entrusted with a very important role.”

“Kiiiiiiii!”

“This bastard bares his teeth at people? Maybe he needs to die to come to his senses.”

Baek Ah, face flushed with fever, snarled, but hearing Chung Myung’s voice she sulked again and scratched the ground.

Seol So-Baek, staring blankly at that sight, nodded as if he understood.

“...I see.”

Here are those who treat spiritual beasts like objects, and those who truly treat them like people.

Same words, but the meaning is... really different. Yes. Very different.

“Then the remaining place is...”

All eyes turned to one place.

And the one who received that gaze answered immediately as if already prepared.

“Beon Chung! Beon Chung will go.”

Im So-Byeong’s face was desperate.

“If an Evil Sect bastard goes there in the first place it will only give a bad impression; what good impression could there be? Opting out would be best, but if you don’t like that I’ll send Beon Chung. He’s strong and would be useful even as a porter!”

“...”

“If not, it’s fine. I’ll immediately prepare a detailed roster of the Ten Shadows of the Green Forest and the Green Forest elders! Choose anyone from them! Whoever you pick I will send at once...”

“Hey.”

“...”

Chung Myung said indifferently.

“Stop talking nonsense and you come too.”

“W-why would the Evil Sect go to the Southern Island...”

“Ah. I understand, so come.”

“...It will only worsen their reputation...”

“Enough, just come.”

“...”

“Let the Green Forest be represented by the Green Forest King.”

In that moment it was decided that Im So-Byeong would join regardless of his own wishes.

“Then the remaining place is...”

Everyone’s gaze turned to Tang Gunak.

Because Mount Hua, the Namgung Family, Green Forest, and the Five Palaces Beyond the Great Wall had each chosen representatives for two of them, the only place left was the Tang family.

“Lord of Tang family?”

Noticing all the eyes on him, Tang Gunak cleared his throat.

“As for the Tang family...”

“Will you go?”

“In the other places, the sect leaders are going in person.”

“...”

A twitch passed across Tang Gunak’s eyes.

“Now... ahem. I am not here as the lord of the Tang family but as the deputy leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, tidying up the situation, so matters related to the Tang family should be directed to the young head, not me.”

“Isn’t that the same thing?”

“Mount Hua also distinguishes between the Acting Sect Leader and the Alliance Leader, so surely the Tang family should do the same.”

It felt like he was trying to back out, but it was awkward to press him on it. Naturally, everyone’s attention shifted to Tang Pae, who was sitting in a corner.

“Uh... well...”

Under the piercing stares, Tang Pae spoke bashfully.

“Of course, the Tang family has many excellent people who could represent it, but...”

Before he could finish, the Five Swords started whispering.

“Many?”

“There used to be. But the Family lord shoved all those people into the back rooms when he teamed up with Chung Myung.”

“Ah, you mean the closure of the council. Then there aren’t any.”

Tang Pae tried to ignore those comments and spoke again.

“But... in terms of balance between sects and symbolic meaning, it would be appropriate for the Lord of the Tang family to go in person...”

At that moment, Tang Pae felt a chill run through him and abruptly turned his head. Tang Gunak’s face flashed into something fierce for an instant before he quickly returned to a blank expression.

“...”

“In person?”

“Ah, no...”

Tang Pae broke into a cold sweat and continued.

“It would be proper, but... as you know, if our Lord goes, it could cause some problems...”

“What kind of problems?”

“...there would be something, I suppose?”

“Pardon?”

“...”

Pressured once again by the hostile atmosphere, Tang Pae groped for words and continued haltingly.

“Well... our Master currently functions as the de facto deputy leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. He has so many duties that if he leaves his post, various problems would arise in the work...”

Jo Gul and Yoon Jong began whispering again.

“Sahyung. Is the Heavenly Friends Alliance doing anything right now?”

“...I wouldn’t know. But there shouldn’t be anything pressing right now, I think.”

“Then how would a vacancy cause problems?”

“Who knows?”

Cold sweat began to bead on Tang Pae’s forehead.

Those damned jackal-like brats. They call themselves Taoists...

“S-so if the family lord leaves his post, the Alliance Leader might struggle.”

“Ah. Is that so.”

“That I can agree with.”

“The Sect Leader must have much on his mind these days; we shouldn’t disturb him.”

Tang Pae ground his teeth. Those damn Mount Hua fellows...

Baek Cheon, as if tired of the long discussion, asked directly for the point.

“So who will the Tang family send?”

“One of the elders...”

“An elder?”

“...”
